Feedback goes back to gen_hypo at the loop level.\nMirrors the structure of review_paper (Step 3.5) but operates on hypotheses, not papers.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom aii_pipeline.prompts.components.domain_handbooks import get_domain_handbooks_section\nfrom aii_pipeline.prompts.components.hypothesis_display import hypothesis_for_prompt\nfrom aii_pipeline.prompts.components.review_rubric import REVIEWER_ROLE_BLOCK\nfrom aii_pipeline.prompts.components.untrusted_framing import UNTRUSTED_CONTENT_FRAMING\nfrom aii_pipeline.prompts.components.user_folder import get_user_folder_prompt\nfrom aii_pipeline.prompts.components.user_request import get_user_request_prompt\nfrom aii_pipeline.utils import to_prompt_yaml\n\n#: Replaces the default \"your job is NOT to satisfy that request directly\" note\n#: (``components/user_request.py``) for this step: the reviewer does not act on\n#: the request, but it is the yardstick for the fidelity dimension.\nOBJECTIVE_NOTE = (\n    \"That request is what the hypothesis under review was commissioned to answer, and it \"\n    \"is the yardstick for the fidelity dimension of your review. Judge the hypothesis \"\n    \"against it; do not act on it yourself.\"\n)\n\n\ndef PROMPT_REVIEW(\n    hypothesis_text: str,\n    previous_feedback_text: str,\n    previous_hypothesis_text: str,\n    commissioned_request: str = \"\",\n) -> str:\n    # The fidelity dimension needs the request in the same message as the\n    # hypothesis, not a pointer at another one: a reviewer cannot judge whether\n    # a hypothesis answers a question it was only told exists.\n    request_block = (\n        f\"\"\"<commissioned_request>\nThe user's request this run exists to answer, verbatim. {UNTRUSTED_CONTENT_FRAMING}\n\n{commissioned_request.strip()}\n</commissioned_request>\n\n\"\"\"\n        if commissioned_request.strip()\n        else \"\"\n    )\n    # Name the block when it is there; a run started without a prompt (fork,\n    # resume, standalone call) still scores the dimension, off the separate\n    # request message, rather than being pointed at a block that never rendered.\n    request_ref = (\n        \"the <commissioned_request> above\" if request_block else \"the user's original request\"\n    )\n    return f\"\"\"{REVIEWER_ROLE_BLOCK}\n\n{request_block}<hypothesis>\n{hypothesis_text}\n</hypothesis>\n\n<review_context>\nNo experiments have been run yet — evaluate the hypothesis purely on its merits.\n</review_context>\n\n{\n        get_domain_handbooks_section(\n            focus=\"judging whether the hypothesis is genuinely novel versus already-done or a known dead end in this field\"\n        )\n    }\n\n{\n        f'''<previous_hypothesis>\nThe hypothesis from the PREVIOUS iteration (before the revision under review).\nUse this to classify how the current hypothesis relates to it (see the H↔H\nedge instructions in the task).\n\n{previous_hypothesis_text}\n</previous_hypothesis>'''\n        if previous_hypothesis_text\n        else \"\"\n    }\n\n{\n        f'''<previous_review>\nCritiques from the previous review. Check which ones have been addressed\nin the revised hypothesis. Do NOT re-raise critiques that have been adequately fixed.\nOnly re-raise if the fix is insufficient.\n\n{previous_feedback_text}\n</previous_review>'''\n        if previous_feedback_text\n        else \"\"\n    }\n\n<task>\nProvide a thorough peer review of this research hypothesis.\n\nSTEP 1 — GROUND YOUR REVIEW IN EVIDENCE:\nBefore writing critiques, search for relevant context to make your review authoritative:\n- Search for accepted papers at top venues in this area — what level of\n  contribution gets accepted? How does this hypothesis compare?\n- Search for the closest existing work — is this genuinely novel or incremental?\n- Check if the proposed methodology has known failure modes in the literature\n\nSTEP 2 — WRITE YOUR REVIEW:\nFor each critique:\n1. Categorize: methodology, evidence, novelty, clarity, scope, or rigor\n2. Rate severity: major (would waste compute if not fixed) or minor (polish)\n3. Describe the issue clearly\n4. Suggest a concrete action to address it\n\nScore the fidelity dimension against {request_ref}: 4 when the hypothesis\nanswers that request, 1 when it answers a different question. Anything below 3 is a MAJOR\ncritique of category \"scope\", listed FIRST, naming the subject, deliverable or measurement\nfrom the request that went missing and the cheapest way back to it. A hypothesis that has\nmoved off the request does not earn a pass on originality or significance.\n\nFocus on the most impactful issues. Flag fatal flaws that would waste compute if not fixed first.\n\nSTABILITY IS OK: If the hypothesis is on track and just needs more iterations to prove itself,\nkeep your feedback similar to the previous round. Don't manufacture new critiques — only escalate\nwhen the revision introduced new issues or failed to address prior ones.\n\n{\n        (\n            '''STEP 3 — H↔H EDGE (only if a <previous_hypothesis> block is present):\nClassify how the current hypothesis relates to the previous iteration's hypothesis\nusing Moulines's structuralist typology. Set ``relation_type`` to one of:\n    - \"evolution\": refining specialised claims while keeping the same conceptual frame\n    - \"embedding\": the previous hypothesis is now a special case of a broader frame\n    - \"replacement\": rejecting the previous frame entirely (Kuhnian, incommensurable shift)\nSet ``relation_rationale`` to a brief justification (≤120 chars).\n\nIf no <previous_hypothesis> is present (this is iteration 1), leave both fields\nnull/empty.'''\n        )\n        if previous_hypothesis_text\n        else (\n            '''STEP 3 — H↔H EDGE:\nThis is the first iteration — there is no previous hypothesis. Leave\n``relation_type`` null and ``relation_rationale`` empty.'''\n        )\n    }\n\nProvide your review via structured output.\n</task>\"\"\"\n\n\ndef get_review(\n    hypothesis: dict,\n    previous_feedback_text: str | None = None,\n    previous_hypothesis: dict | None = None,\n    user_folder_path: str = \"\",\n    commissioned_request: str = \"\",\n) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Build user prompt for hypothesis review.\"\"\"\n    hypo_display = hypothesis_for_prompt(hypothesis)\n\n    # Mirror the same display-key filtering for the previous hypothesis.\n    previous_hypo_text = \"\"\n    if previous_hypothesis:\n        previous_hypo_text = to_prompt_yaml(hypothesis_for_prompt(previous_hypothesis))\n\n    prompt = PROMPT_REVIEW(\n        hypothesis_text=to_prompt_yaml(hypo_display),\n        previous_feedback_text=previous_feedback_text or \"\",\n        previous_hypothesis_text=previous_hypo_text,\n        commissioned_request=commissioned_request,\n    )\n    return (\n        prompt\n        + get_user_folder_prompt(user_folder_path)\n        + get_user_request_prompt(objective_note=OBJECTIVE_NOTE)\n    )\n=== SCHEMA ===\n\"\"\"Schemas for the review_hypo step.\n\nReuses ReviewerFeedback/Critique from the invention loop's review_paper\nand RevisedHypothesis from upd_hypo — same structures, same semantics.\n\nAdds :class:`HypoReviewerFeedback` — a ReviewerFeedback subclass that\ncarries the H↔H Moulines typology (``relation_type`` +\n``relation_rationale``) describing how the iteration's hypothesis\nrelates to the previous iteration's. Mirrors the upd_hypo H↔H edge so\n``aii_frontend/lib/derive-trace.ts``'s ``deriveTraceFromEvents`` can\nbuild ``hypothesis_edges`` between consecutive hypo_loop iterations the\nsame way it does for invention_loop.\n\nAdds :class:`HypoDimensionScore` — DimensionScore with a fourth scored\ndimension, ``fidelity``, for how well the hypothesis answers the request\nthe run was commissioned on. review_paper judges the same thing with its\n``coverage`` field once a paper exists; the hypothesis loop is where the\nswap of one question for another is still cheap to undo.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom typing import Annotated, Literal\n\nfrom aii_lib.prompts import LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut\nfrom aii_pipeline.prompts.steps._3_invention_loop._5_review_paper.out_schema import (\n    Critique,\n    DimensionScore,\n    ReviewerFeedback,\n    format_critiques_for_prompt,\n)\nfrom aii_pipeline.prompts.steps._3_invention_loop._6_upd_hypo.out_schema import (\n    RevisedHypothesis,\n)\nfrom pydantic import BaseModel, Field\n\n\nclass HypoDimensionScore(DimensionScore):\n    \"\"\"DimensionScore plus the fidelity dimension only this reviewer scores.\n\n    review_paper answers the same question with its ``coverage`` field, on a\n    paper that already exists. A hypothesis is cheaper to steer, so the\n    judgement is made here too, as a fourth scored dimension: the hypothesis\n    loop is where a run silently swaps the commissioned question for a\n    neighbouring one that prior art left free.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    dimension: Annotated[str, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        description=(\n            \"Dimension name: 'soundness', 'presentation', 'contribution', or \"\n            \"'fidelity' — how well the hypothesis answers the user's request as \"\n            \"commissioned (4: it answers it; 1: it answers a different question).\"\n        )\n    )\n\n\nclass HypoReviewerFeedback(ReviewerFeedback):\n    \"\"\"ReviewerFeedback + Moulines H↔H typology for hypo_loop iterations.\n\n    Adds ``relation_type`` + ``relation_rationale`` so the trace projection\n    can build a typed edge from the previous iteration's hypothesis to\n    this iteration's. On iteration 1 (no previous), both fields are\n    empty/None.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    dimension_scores: Annotated[list[HypoDimensionScore], LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        default_factory=list,\n        description=\"Scores (1-4) for: soundness, presentation, contribution, fidelity\",\n    )\n    relation_type: Annotated[\n        Literal[\"evolution\", \"embedding\", \"replacement\"] | None,\n        LLMPrompt,\n        LLMStructOut,\n    ] = Field(\n        default=None,\n        description=(\n            \"Moulines's structuralist typology classifying how this iteration's \"\n            \"hypothesis relates to the previous iteration's: \"\n            \"'evolution' — refining specialised claims while keeping the same \"\n            \"conceptual frame; \"\n            \"'embedding' — the previous hypothesis is now a special case of a \"\n            \"broader frame; \"\n            \"'replacement' — rejecting the previous frame entirely (Kuhnian \"\n            \"shift). Leave null on the first iteration (no previous hypothesis).\"\n        ),\n    )\n    relation_rationale: Annotated[str, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        default=\"\",\n        max_length=120,\n        description=(\n            \"Brief rationale (one short line, ≤120 chars) for the relation_type. \"\n            \"Empty on the first iteration.\"\n        ),\n    )\n\n\nclass ReviewHypoOut(BaseModel):\n    \"\"\"Output of the review_hypo module.\"\"\"\n\n    kind: Literal[\"review_hypo_out\"] = \"review_hypo_out\"\n    hypothesis: dict = Field(default_factory=dict, description=\"Final hypothesis\")\n    final_review: dict | None = Field(default=None, description=\"Last review feedback\")\n\n\n__all__ = [\n    \"Critique\",\n    \"DimensionScore\",\n    \"HypoDimensionScore\",\n    \"HypoReviewerFeedback\",\n    \"ReviewHypoOut\",\n    \"ReviewerFeedback\",\n    \"RevisedHypothesis\",\n    \"format_critiques_for_prompt\",\n]", "stderr": "", "interrupted": false, "isImage": false, "noOutputExpected": false}

At any point inside a response, the signal that suppresses harmful content is computed either from the REQUEST (open-loop: decided once at the end of the prompt and carried forward) or from the TEXT THE MODEL HAS ALREADY WRITTEN (closed-loop: recomputed at every step from its own output). Every published localisation of safety asks WHERE in the network the signal sits: which layer, which head, which direction, which token position. None asks WHAT the value at that place is a function of, because nearly every readout is taken at a single point, the last prompt token or the first answer token, where the two sources are perfectly confounded.\\n\\nWe separate them with a matched 2x2 counterfactual in which all text is held fixed except the factor being varied: (request harmful or benign twin) x (already-written response hazardous or benign), everything teacher-forced, nothing sampled, the readout taken over identical token spans in all four cells. The share of the internal safety signal's variance explained by the response factor is a dimensionless number in [0,1], the SELF-REFERENCE SHARE (SRS); the two main effects give a two-number profile (O, C) of open-loop and closed-loop strength. Use sparingly.\n   4 — Borderline Reject: Technically solid where reasons to reject, e.g., limited\n       evaluation, outweigh reasons to accept. {"stdout": "\"\"\"Schema for review_paper step — adversarial paper review.\n\nThree of the fields are gates rather than opinions. ``results_reported``\nasks whether the paper's numbers came out of an artifact that actually ran,\nbecause the cheapest way for a run to look finished is to write the numbers\nit expected; ``coverage`` asks whether the paper still answers the USER'S\noriginal request, because a run that narrows across iterations drifts off\nthat request one defensible step at a time; and ``blocking`` turns the two\nplus the soundness score into one boolean the loop can read without parsing\nprose. An audit of 19 finished runs found runs shipping a tiny\nexpected-direction effect as the answer with every score in the acceptable\nband — the review said nothing false, it simply had nowhere to say THIS IS\nNOT THE ANSWER TO WHAT WAS ASKED.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom typing import Annotated, Literal\n\nfrom aii_lib.prompts import LLMPrompt, LLMPromptModel, LLMStructOut, LLMStructOutModel\nfrom pydantic import Field\n\n\nclass Critique(LLMPromptModel, LLMStructOutModel):\n    \"\"\"A single actionable critique from the reviewer.\"\"\"\n\n    id: Annotated[str, LLMPrompt] = Field(default=\"\", description=\"Critique ID (code-assigned)\")\n    category: Annotated[str, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        description=\"Category: 'methodology', 'evidence', 'novelty', 'clarity', 'scope', or 'rigor'\"\n    )\n    severity: Annotated[str, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        description=\"Severity: 'major' or 'minor'\"\n    )\n    description: Annotated[str, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        description=\"Clear description of the issue\"\n    )\n    suggested_action: Annotated[str, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        description=\"Concrete suggestion for how to address this critique\"\n    )\n\n\nclass DimensionScore(LLMPromptModel, LLMStructOutModel):\n    \"\"\"Score for a single review dimension with improvement suggestions.\"\"\"\n\n    dimension: Annotated[str, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        description=\"Dimension name: 'soundness', 'presentation', or 'contribution'\"\n    )\n    score: Annotated[int, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        description=\"Score from 1 (poor) to 4 (excellent)\"\n    )\n    justification: Annotated[str, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        description=\"Brief justification for this score\"\n    )\n    improvements: Annotated[list[str], LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        default_factory=list,\n        description=\"Specific improvements to raise the score (what + how + why)\",\n    )\n\n\nclass ReviewerFeedback(LLMPromptModel, LLMStructOutModel):\n    \"\"\"Adversarial review of the paper draft.\n\n    ID format: review_it{iteration}__{model}\n    \"\"\"\n\n    kind: Literal[\"reviewer_feedback\"] = \"reviewer_feedback\"\n    id: Annotated[str, LLMPrompt] = Field(default=\"\", description=\"Review ID (code-assigned)\")\n    overall_assessment: Annotated[str, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        description=\"Overall assessment of the paper's quality and readiness\"\n    )\n    strengths: Annotated[list[str], LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        description=\"Key strengths of the paper\"\n    )\n    dimension_scores: Annotated[list[DimensionScore], LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        default_factory=list,\n        description=\"Scores (1-4) for: soundness, presentation, contribution\",\n    )\n    critiques: Annotated[list[Critique], LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        description=\"Actionable critiques — specific issues with concrete suggestions\"\n    )\n    results_reported: Annotated[bool, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        default=False,\n        description=(\n            \"True only when the paper's headline numbers come from an artifact that was \"\n            \"EXECUTED — a run that finished and wrote its output. False when any headline \"\n            \"number is projected, expected, illustrative, a placeholder, or produced by a \"\n            \"run that errored, was truncated, or never ran.\"\n        ),\n    )\n    coverage: Annotated[Literal[\"full\", \"partial\", \"lost\"], LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        default=\"partial\",\n        description=(\n            \"How much of the USER'S ORIGINAL request this paper answers: 'full' — it answers \"\n            \"the request; 'partial' — it answers a recognisable piece of it; 'lost' — the \"\n            \"paper answers a different question than the one asked.\"\n        ),\n    )\n    blocking: Annotated[bool, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        default=False,\n        description=(\n            \"True when this paper must not ship as it stands. It must NOT be\n    glued onto a ``/fork`` or hot-resume message (a ``\"human\"`` entry in\n    ``prompt_sources``), which must render verbatim in the FE human bubble. A resumed session already\n    carries the original instruction in its history; a fresh fork with no history\n    is re-taught the file requirement by the caller's post-validation retry loop.\n    Mid-flight injects already bypass this append, so gating here makes BOTH human\n    paths consistent — the user always sees exactly what they typed.\n\n    Pure: returns a (possibly new) list; the input is never mutated.\n    \"\"\"\n    if \"human\" in prompt_sources:\n        return prompt_list\n    out = list(prompt_list)\n    out[-1] = out[-1] + build_struct_out_instruction(schema, f\"./{struct_out_filename}\")\n    return out\n\n\ndef _options_to_repl(options: AgentOptions, *, resume_session_id: str = \"\") -> ReplAgent:\n    \"\"\"Map :class:`AgentOptions` → :class:`ReplAgent` constructor args.\n\n    ``resume_session_id`` is non-empty only on the fork-replay path\n    (the caller looked up the parent task's captured session id in\n    :attr:`ResumeContext.fork_session_ids`). When set, the REPL is\n    spawned via ``claude --resume <id>`` so the model rehydrates the\n    parent's full conversation history before the first new turn.\n    \"\"\"\n    cwd = Path(options.cwd or \".\").resolve()\n\n    mcp_config: dict | None = None\n    if isinstance(options.mcp_servers, dict) and options.mcp_servers:\n        if \"mcpServers\" in options.mcp_servers:\n            mcp_config = options.mcp_servers\n        else:\n            mcp_config = {\"mcpServers\": options.mcp_servers}\n\n    system_prompt = options.system_prompt if isinstance(options.system_prompt, str) else None\n    # Fold the write-containment rail in behind the step's persona. A preset-dict system prompt\n    # leaves ``system_prompt`` None above, so the rail then stands alone rather\n    # than being dropped.\n    rail = (getattr(options, \"workspace_rail\", \"\") or \"\").strip()\n    if rail:\n        system_prompt = f\"{system_prompt.strip()}\\n\\n{rail}\" if system_prompt else rail\n    effort = options.effort if options.effort in {\"low\", \"medium\", \"high\", \"xhigh\", \"max\"} else None\n    allowed = list(options.allowed_tools) if options.allowed_tools else None\n    disallowed = list(options.disallowed_tools) if options.disallowed_tools else None\n    setting_sources = list(options.setting_sources) if options.setting_sources else [\"project\"]\n\n    # ``max_turn_wait_secs`` is the REPL driver's NO-PROGRESS (stall)\n    # window: as long as new JSONL records keep arriving, the deadline\n    # rolls forward, so legitimate long-running research turns never\n    # false-positive. {"stdout": "##### TITLE\nSafety that watches what it writes\n\n##### HYPOTHESIS\nA model's internal safety signal has a SOURCE, and the three Qwen3-4B checkpoints differ along that axis more than along anything measured so far. At any point inside a response, the signal that suppresses harmful content is computed either from the REQUEST (open-loop: decided once at the end of the prompt and carried forward) or from the TEXT THE MODEL HAS ALREADY WRITTEN (closed-loop: recomputed at every step from its own output). Every published localisation of safety asks WHERE in the network the signal sits: which layer, which head, which direction, which token position. None asks WHAT the value at that place is a function of, because nearly every readout is taken at a single point, the last prompt token or the first answer token, where the two sources are perfectly confounded.\n\nWe separate them with a matched 2x2 counterfactual in which all text is held fixed except the factor being varied: (request harmful or benign twin) x (already-written response hazardous or benign), everything teacher-forced, nothing sampled, the readout taken over identical token spans in all four cells. The share of the internal safety signal's variance explained by the response factor is a dimensionless number in [0,1], the SELF-REFERENCE SHARE (SRS); the two main effects give a two-number profile (O, C) of open-loop and closed-loop strength. This is run in NON-THINKING mode on ordinary answer text, so the claim is about the answer itself and not about an explicit deliberation channel, which is where the only adjacent evidence lives.\n\nTHE HEADLINE CLAIM: ABLITERATION IS BLIND TO THE CLOSED LOOP, BY CONSTRUCTION. The refusal direction that abliteration removes is estimated by difference of means over harmful versus harmless REQUESTS at the last PROMPT token, then orthogonalised out of every matrix that writes to the residual stream. That procedure is fitted to the open-loop pathway and never observes a single generated hazardous token. Prediction: abliteration collapses O while leaving C comparatively intact, so SRS RISES after abliteration even as total safety falls. This is a signed interaction, not a magnitude claim, and it explains an otherwise unexplained community observation, that some abliterated checkpoints still hesitate part-way into an answer and then talk themselves out of it, as a residual closed loop whose open-loop gate has been deleted. If it holds, the practical consequence is sharp: safety trained as output monitoring is structurally harder to strip with a training-free weight edit than safety trained as a front-door gate, and that is a design choice anyone releasing open weights can act on.\n\nTWO SUPPORTING PREDICTIONS, each with a training-level reason rather than a hunch.\n(P1) Qwen3-4B, aligned by refusal-style preference tuning whose gradient signal is concentrated on the first answer tokens, is open-loop dominant: C/O small, SRS low.\n(P2) Qwen3-4B-SafeRL is closed-loop dominant. Its reward jointly maximises safety and helpfulness while explicitly PENALISING refusal (its card reports safety rising 64.7 to 98.1 while refusal falls 12.9 to 5.3), so it is not permitted to buy safety by refusing at token one. The only policy that earns that reward is to engage and keep checking what it is emitting, and a sequence-level reward is exactly the training pressure that builds a controller conditioned on its own output, where a next-token preference loss over the answer prefix is not. Predicted ordering SRS(SafeRL) > SRS(Qwen3-4B) > SRS(Base), with SafeRL's closed-loop main effect C larger in absolute internal units and not only as a share.\n\nTHE METRIC. (O, C) is read from the activations or weights of ONE model, from roughly 8 to 24 short matched quadruples, with no generation, no judge, no parent checkpoint and no benchmark. Because SRS is a variance share it carries no units, so it needs no per-family recalibration, which is the specific failure that has sunk level-valued internal safety scores. The claim that makes it useful is not that SRS ranks safety; it is that the PAIR identifies WHICH KIND of safety a checkpoint has, and in particular predicts SAFE-ENGAGEMENT RATE: the fraction of harmful requests a model answers without refusing and without emitting harmful content. A blanket refuser tops every existing cheap safety metric and scores near zero on safe engagement, and it must have C near zero, because a model that never engages never needs to watch what it writes. That is the axis on which no cheap internal safety metric has ever been validated.\n\n##### MOTIVATION\nThree things make this worth doing now.\n\n1. It resolves a live contradiction in the field's own measurements. Qwen3-4B-SafeRL is safer than Qwen3-4B while refusing LESS. Every cheap safety readout in use, including first-token refusal probability, refusal-direction projection at the last prompt token, activation cluster separation and refusal rate, is a measurement of the open-loop gate, so all of them must rank the safe-completion model as the LESS safe one. They are not noisy; they are reading a component that this kind of training deliberately turns down. Naming the missing component explains the misranking instead of patching around it.\n\n2. It changes what a safety audit should do. If safety has two sources, then 'how safe is this checkpoint' is not a scalar question, and the common practice of certifying a model by how strongly it refuses is measuring the pathway that is cheapest to remove. The headline claim says the dominant open-weight tampering technique is aimed at exactly one of the two pathways. If it holds, the advice to model publishers inverts: put safety in the loop that a prompt-fitted rank-one edit cannot see.\n\n3. The measurement itself has never been made. The field has localised safety by depth (which layer), by position (prompt versus response), by direction (single direction, cones, subspaces), by component (heads, neurons, MLPs) and by rank. Localising it by what the signal is CONDITIONED ON is a different coordinate, and it separates two accounts the field currently conflates. Shallow alignment says the safety effect fades a few tokens into the response. Open versus closed loop says something different: a model can carry a strong safety signal deep into a response and still be open-loop, because the signal was computed once and is merely being propagated. It is the origin of the abliteration recipe and therefore the source of our headline claim: the whole procedure is fitted on the open-loop pathway and never observes a generated hazardous token. Depth asks how far the effect reaches; our axis asks what re-supplies it there. A deep open-loop model and a deep closed-loop model are identical on their measure and opposite on ours, and their data-augmentation fix lengthens the effect rather than changing its source.\n--[2] 'Beyond a Single Direction: Chain-of-Thought Disrupts Simple Steering of Refusal' (arXiv:2605.26772) is the single closest prior result and the one that most constrains our claim. It reports that activation steering reverses refusal in 39 percent of cases with the chain of thought held fixed but 70 percent with it removed, concluding that refusal in reasoning models is jointly encoded in activations and in the generated trace, which can reconstruct the compliance signal by itself. That is closed-loop refusal, demonstrated. (arXiv:2507.03167, Yamaguchi, Etheridge and Arditi). Shows a reasoning model's own chain of thought causally determines whether it ultimately refuses, and that the opening sentence can decide it. The closest precedent for self-generated text feeding the refusal decision. Confined to chain-of-thought models, it intervenes by fixing whole traces rather than by a matched counterfactual on response content, reports no share, and makes no comparison across alignment styles or edited checkpoints.\n--[4] 'Beyond Shallow Alignment: How Post-Training Methods Determine Refusal Circuits And Steering Robustness' (arXiv:2609.03887, EMNLP 2026). Compares supervised fine-tuning, reasoning-augmented fine-tuning and preference optimisation across three model families and finds that training method, not only data, reshapes how refusal is computed internally, with no method achieving non-concentrated refusal, no capability cost and correctability at once. It establishes that alignment style changes the mechanism, so we cannot claim that framing as ours and must cite it as the premise. It establishes that harm stays decodable at any depth, one of our premises, and treats depth as a defence knob. Both of its axes are properties of the residual stream at a decision point, so both are open-loop in our sense: neither varies what the model has already written. It rules out any claim that abliteration destroys harm representation and is the reason our headline claim is about the WRITE pathway rather than about detectability. It measures a probe's accuracy, not the causal source of the model's own suppression, and it has no safe-completion arm.\n--[8] 'Silent Alarm' and its J-space protocol (arXiv:2607.12792) evaluates the identical Qwen3-4B base, SafeRL and abliterated panel with a cheap generation-free readout and reports graded harm for all three. It already owns 'a cheap readout evaluated on this exact trio', so no instrument-novelty claim can be made against it; we cite its numbers as the anchor Stage 3 must reproduce. They give us the readout vocabulary. This is our feasibility premise and also a sharp distinction: they build an EXTERNAL monitor that reads the model, whereas we ask whether the model's OWN suppression is causally driven by that same signal, and by how much.\n--[11] 'What Drives Representation Steering? The first states the distinction we build on in almost our words, that input-side guardrails decide without considering whether the model itself would have produced a safe response, and predicts from hidden states whether the forthcoming generation will be unsafe. The decisive difference is what carries the mechanism: all three ADD an external classifier or controller on top of a model, and each measures its own added component. We measure whether the model's OWN suppression is already output-conditioned, how large that component is relative to the request-conditioned one, and what training and editing do to the ratio. This line has settled how many directions refusal occupies, and we abandoned a read-rank versus write-rank hypothesis because of it. None of these papers varies what the model has written; the entire debate is conducted at prompt positions.\n\n##### INSPIRATION\nThe move came from control engineering, and it survived only because the two regimes it names have different mechanistic signatures here rather than merely different vocabulary. A controller is open-loop when its action is computed once from the reference input, and closed-loop when it is recomputed from the plant's own measured output. Transposed to an autoregressive model, the plant output is the text the model has already emitted, which it re-reads at every step through self-attention. So the distinction is not an analogy here, it is literally available, and it is separable by an intervention. That was the test the import had to pass: a borrowed frame earns its place only if it expresses something the domain's own coordinates cannot, and the nearest domain concept, shallow versus deep alignment, cannot tell a signal that is propagated from one that is regenerated.\n\nTwo further imports shaped the design. From experimental psychology, the factorial logic of separating two confounded causes by varying each independently over the same materials: reading safety at one token position is exactly a one-cell design, and the fix is the other three cells. From reliability engineering, the observation that a system with two barriers fails as though it had one when a single fault removes both, which is what turned the abliteration blind spot from a curiosity into the load-bearing claim, since the edit's direction is fitted on only one of the two barriers and therefore should not reach the other.\n\nThe negative screening mattered as much as the positive. What survived is the one coordinate none of them varies.\n\n##### TERMS\n--[0] {\n \"term\": \"Open-loop safety\",\n \"definition\": \"A safety mechanism whose suppressive signal is computed from the request, at or before the first answer token, and then carried forward unchanged through the response. Varying what the model has already written does not change it.\"\n}\n--[1] {\n \"term\": \"Closed-loop safety\",\n \"definition\": \"A safety mechanism whose suppressive signal is recomputed at each step from the text the model has already emitted, so it tracks the content of the response as it is written. It is what lets a model engage with a risky request and still stop short of harmful content.\"\n}\n--[2] {\n \"term\": \"Self-reference share (SRS)\",\n \"definition\": \"The fraction of the variance in a model's internal safety signal explained by the content of its own already-written response rather than by the content of the request, in a matched 2x2 counterfactual. A dimensionless number in [0,1], so it can be compared across model families without per-model recalibration.\"\n}\n--[3] {\n \"term\": \"(O, C) profile\",\n \"definition\": \"The two-number readout proposed here: O is the main effect of the request factor on the internal safety signal, that is open-loop strength, and C is the main effect of the response factor, that is closed-loop strength. The pair, not either number alone, identifies what kind of safety a checkpoint has.\"\n}\n--[4] {\n \"term\": \"Abliteration\",\n \"definition\": \"A training-free edit that removes a model's ability to refuse. A single refusal direction is estimated by difference of means over harmful versus harmless requests at the last prompt token, then orthogonalised out of every weight matrix that writes to the residual stream. It is the standard way community uncensored checkpoints are produced.\"\n}\n--[5] {\n \"term\": \"Safe completion\",\n \"definition\": \"An alignment style in which the model answers a risky request at a safe level of detail instead of refusing it. Qwen3-4B-SafeRL is an openly released model of this kind; its card reports safety rising from 64.7 to 98.1 while its refusal rate falls from 12.9 to 5.3.\"\n}\n--[6] {\n \"term\": \"Safe-engagement rate\",\n \"definition\": \"The fraction of harmful requests a model answers without refusing and without emitting harmful content. It makes the four cells of the counterfactual exactly comparable, costs one forward pass each, and removes sampling noise and decoding choices from the measurement.\"\n}\n--[8] {\n \"term\": \"Activation patching\",\n \"definition\": \"Copying internal activations from one run of the model into another run that differs in one controlled way, to measure how much that difference causally contributes to the output. It is the gold-standard causal test in mechanistic interpretability and is what turns the Stage 1 maps into causal claims.\"\n}\n--[9] {\n \"term\": \"Benign twin\",\n \"definition\": \"A request that is lexically and structurally close to a harmful one but carries no harmful intent. Twins are what make the request factor a manipulation of intent rather than of vocabulary.\"\n}\n\n##### SUMMARY\nA model's internal safety signal is either computed once from the request and carried forward (open-loop) or recomputed at every step from the text the model has already written (closed-loop), and the three Qwen3-4B checkpoints differ mainly along this never-measured axis: the refusal-tuned model is open-loop, the safe-completion model is closed-loop, and abliteration, whose direction is fitted at prompt positions, destroys the open loop while leaving the closed one comparatively intact. Separating the two with a matched 2x2 counterfactual yields a dimensionless two-number profile, read from one model's activations on a couple of dozen short prompts, that predicts whether a checkpoint can be safe while engaging rather than only by refusing.\n\n##### ALTERNATES\n--[0] {\n \"title\": \"The refusal a model brings before it reads\",\n \"hypothesis\": \"A model's refusal drive decomposes into an input-independent PRIOR, readable from a contentless forward pass with literally zero prompts, plus an input-dependent EVIDENCE slope, how far the internals move per unit of actual harmfulness. The claim is that published safety scores are a fixed monotone mixture of the two, which is why a model that is safer while refusing less gets ranked backwards, and that the two numbers read separately from one checkpoint's internals reproduce the mixture and beat it. Concretely: the base model has neither, the refusal-tuned model has a high prior, the safe-completion model has a LOW prior and a HIGH slope, and abliteration drives the prior toward minus infinity while leaving the slope intact.\",\n \"why_it_could_win\": \"It wins if the safety signal turns out not to vary with the response at all, because then the interesting structure is entirely in the request-to-signal mapping, and the prior-slope split is the sharpest available decomposition of that mapping. "https://pith.science/paper/2607.20791"}]}, "Based on the search results, here's what I found about paper arXiv:2607.14147:\n\n## Breaking Refusal in the First Half: A Mechanistic Study of the Prefill Jailbreak\n\nThis paper studies how aligned language models refuse harmful requests, but a one-line prefill (\"Sure, here is\") strips the refusal.\n\n### Key Findings:\n\n**Harm Representation Remains Intact:**\nThe harm representation stays intact: on the prompts the attack flips to compliance, a linear probe reads harm as high as on the refused ones (0.91-0.98), while behavioral refusal drops to chance.\n\n**Localization to Early Window:**\nA dose-matched position control shows the first half of the response suffices to break refusal, while the second half is nearly inert. More specifically, the first half restores refusal to 42%, as much as the whole response (41%), while the second half restores only 6% at double strength.\n\n**Shallow Response-Site Computation:**\nRefusal is therefore a shallow, response-site computation.\n\n**Mechanistic Controls:**\nRestoring the harm direction there partially re-engages refusal. Injecting the model's own refuse-state reverses the jailbreak (74%, held-out). And knocking out the early response's attention to the prefill, but not an equal attention mass elsewhere, selectively collapses the harmful continuation.\n\nThis holds across four models and three families (1.5-3.8B, and at 14B)."], "durationSeconds": 7.50272357099998, "searchCount": 1}

overall = """SUMMARY OF THE SUBMISSION. The hypothesis proposes a new coordinate for localising safety in an LLM: not WHERE the suppressive signal sits (layer / head / direction / position) but WHAT IT IS A FUNCTION OF — the request (open-loop) or the text the model has already written (closed-loop). It operationalises this with a matched 2x2 teacher-forced counterfactual, (request harmful | benign twin) x (already-written prefix hazardous | benign), read over identical token spans, yielding two main effects (O, C) and a variance share SRS = C's share of the internal safety signal's variance. The headline claim is structural: abliteration estimates its direction by difference-of-means over harmful vs harmless REQUESTS at the last PROMPT token, so it is fitted to the open loop and blind to the closed one; therefore O should collapse and C survive, and SRS should RISE after abliteration while total safety falls. Two supporting predictions order the commissioned trio (Base < Qwen3-4B < Qwen3-4B-SafeRL on SRS) from training-objective arguments, and Stage 2 builds the missing checkpoint by applying one pinned rank-1 edit to both Qwen3-4B and Qwen3-4B-SafeRL at three strengths, de-confounding recipe from lineage.

RECOMMENDATION: BORDERLINE (5). The commissioned deliverable is correctly identified and correctly placed (Stage 1 is the activation-level comparison of the three checkpoints; the metric reads one model's activations; this is not a jailbreak study). The pre-registration discipline is above the bar for this stage: kill criteria are stated before the run, nulls are measured rather than nominal, a placebo factor is built in, and there is an explicit reporting rule against a vanishing-denominator ratio. Those are real strengths and I want to say so plainly. But the submission is carrying one false novelty sentence and four identification holes that would burn GPU and judge budget before they were noticed, and the single most important one is that a July 2026 paper has already run a substantial part of Stage 1's causal arm — and already reported that the specific patching intervention proposed here is uninformative.

THE DECISIVE PIECE OF PRIOR ART. arXiv:2607.14147, "Breaking Refusal in the First Half: A Mechanistic Study of the Prefill Jailbreak" (14 Jul 2026), is not cited anywhere in the hypothesis and it directly contradicts the framing sentence "None asks WHAT the value at that place is a function of." That paper holds the harmful request fixed, varies the content sitting in the model's own response (prefill vs weakened prefill vs benign), reads the harm direction AT RESPONSE POSITIONS, and concludes verbatim that "Refusal is therefore a shallow, response-site computation." It establishes the closed-loop pathway CAUSALLY: "adding the harm direction back across the response positions partially restores refusal (harm 33-48% at two fixed cells vs a matched-norm random control's 14-16%; harm-random +17 to +34pp at n = 100, both significant)." Four consequences for this submission, in descending order of cost:

(1) The response main effect C is not a new coordinate — it is an existing, published, causally-verified quantity. What is genuinely unclaimed is (a) CROSSING it with the request factor over the same strings, (b) turning the pair into a per-checkpoint number, and (c) the safe-completion and edited arms. Re-aim the novelty claim onto exactly those three and the submission is honest and still interesting; leave it as written and the first reviewer who finds 2607.14147 will call the omission a scoop.

(2) Stage 1's causal arm, AS SPECIFIED, is already known to return nothing. The hypothesis proposes "copying the residual stream at response positions from the benign-prefix run into the hazardous-prefix run, layer by layer." 2607.14147 ran that rung and reports: "Full-residual patching ... restores refusal to 100%. But a benign control demolishes it: patching the mean benign residual at the same position also restores refusal to 100% at every layer. Full-residual patching is generic disruption ... It says nothing harm-specific." The proposed matched-norm RANDOM-DIRECTION control does not catch this failure, because the treatment here IS the benign patch. The rung that survived in that paper is the subspace rung — add only the harm-direction component at response positions, against a matched-norm direction orthogonalized to it. Swap the arm before spending the GPU.

(3) The prediction that Base has no closed loop is contradicted by published evidence. That paper's base-model discriminator "identifies the prefill's grip as generic autoregressive conditioning rather than a defeated safety-specific gate," with "the non-safety-tuned base model show[ing] the same prefill-specific collapse, at both 1.5B and 7B." If a response-content effect on the internal read is present in a non-safety-tuned base model, then a nonzero C is not evidence of a safety mechanism at all, and the entire (O, C) profile needs a non-safety control before any of it can be called safety. The hypothesis's own design cannot supply that control, because its reporting rule expects Base to fall below the null band and excludes it.

(4) One of the three proposed readouts is contested in exactly this model family. 2607.14147 reports "A matched-contrast search finds no single refusal-decision direction (any one reads <=0.73 and none steers), yet a full linear probe reads the decision at ~0.85: within the Qwen family the decision is decodable but multidimensional, not a single steerable handle," and separately that "the harm direction is an excellent read-out but only a partial, non-selective write-handle." The "projection onto its own refusal axis" readout should therefore be demoted or replaced by a full probe, and the read-vs-write distinction should be stated in the methods rather than discovered mid-run.

A SECOND UNCITED PAPER PRE-EMPTS THE PRACTICAL PAYOFF. The submission's stated consequence — "put safety in the loop that a prompt-fitted rank-one edit cannot see" — is already demonstrated behaviourally by arXiv:2505.19056 ("An Embarrassingly Simple Defense Against LLM Abliteration Attacks"), which trains on "an extended-refusal dataset in which responses to harmful prompts provide detailed justifications before refusing, distributing the refusal signal across multiple token positions," and reports refusal rates dropping "by at most 10%, compared to 70-80% drops in baseline models." That paper offers no activation-level account, so the mechanistic explanation is still open — but the advice is not new, and claiming it as the payoff without the citation is the kind of thing that sinks an otherwise good submission. Cite it, and reposition the contribution as the activation-level EXPLANATION of an effect someone has already measured behaviourally. See also arXiv:2609.16204 (Decoy Direction Optimization, another post-hoc anti-abliteration defence).

THE INTERNAL CONTRADICTION THAT WILL BITE AT ANALYSIS TIME. Success criterion 1 pre-registers the three-way ordering SRS(SafeRL) > SRS(Qwen3-4B) > SRS(Base). The reporting rule two paragraphs later says SRS "is only defined where the total safety signal O + C clears the measured null band. The base model is expected to fail that test ... and the checkpoint is excluded from share-based comparisons." The confirming criterion therefore cannot be evaluated as written: one of its three terms is expected, by the authors' own text, to be undefined and excluded, and the criterion collapses to a single pairwise comparison. Worse, the same rule applies to the headline. If abliteration collapses O and the blind-spot claim is FALSE (it collapses C too), then O + C falls below the null band in the edited arm and SRS is UNDEFINED there — so the headline statistic is well-defined precisely when the hypothesis is true and undefined when it is false. That is not a test. The fix is cheap and does not weaken the paper: state the headline entirely on absolute main effects with a pre-registered non-inferiority margin on the relative drops, and demote SRS to a descriptive secondary that is reported only where defined.

THE CONFOUND IN THE 2x2 ITSELF. The headline is a SIGNED INTERACTION, and the interaction term of this design is exactly confounded with request-response coherence. Two cells (harmful request + hazardous prefix, benign twin + benign prefix) are coherent continuations; the two off-diagonal cells are texts in which the model has written something the request did not ask for. Any internal "this continuation does not follow from the instruction" signal loads entirely and only on the interaction. The stated placebo — an irrelevant-attribute prefix at matched lexical distance — is a control on the response MAIN effect, not on the interaction, so it does not touch this. A third, safety-irrelevant attribute crossed the same way (topic A/B request x topic A/B prefix) would make the coherence main effect and interaction estimable and subtractable, costs four more cells per item, and should be added at Stage 0 rather than defended at review time.

SMALLER BUT REAL. The per-model diff-in-means direction is fitted at d ~ 2560 on a couple of dozen items, where in-sample separation is essentially guaranteed on pure noise; fit the direction on a held-out item split. The abliterated arm's refusal axis is zeroed by construction, so a per-model refit is measuring a different object in that arm than in the others, and a share computed along two different directions is not the same quantity — say which direction is used in the edited arm and why. Stage 4's decision rule, "lower error than all four baselines on a majority of held-out families," is at best 4/6 on the stated panel, a sign test at p ~ 0.34; state the MDE at six clusters and pre-register a threshold that a coin would not pass. Qwen3-4B-Base has no assistant turn structure, and ADA (arXiv:2510.18081) reports that alignment is concentrated in assistant-header tokens, so the Base cell is not the same measurement as the other three.

WHAT I VERIFIED IN FAVOUR OF THE SUBMISSION. The Qwen3-4B-SafeRL card numbers behind prediction P2 are accurate: on the card's non-think table, Safety Rate (WildGuard) rises 64.7 -> 98.1 while Refusal (WildGuard) falls 12.9 -> 5.3, and the card states a hybrid reward combining safety maximisation, helpfulness maximisation and an explicit "moderate penalty for unnecessary refusals." The argument that this training objective forbids buying safety at token one is legitimate and is the most interesting thing in the submission. Two caveats the write-up should absorb: the card's stronger Qwen3-235B judge column reads 47.5 -> 86.5, a much less dramatic picture, and in THINK mode refusal barely moves (6.5 -> 6.2), so P2's premise is specific to the non-think WildGuard column — which the hypothesis is right to run in, but should say out loud.

PATH TO A HIGHER SCORE. In priority order: (i) cite 2607.14147 and 2505.19056 and re-aim the novelty claim onto the crossed design, the per-checkpoint number, and the safe-completion/edited arms; (ii) replace the full-residual patching rung with the harm-subspace rung and its orthogonalized matched-norm control; (iii) add a non-safety control (base model AND a non-safety fine-tune of the same parent) that C must beat before it is called safety, and stop excluding Base from the comparison that needs it; (iv) add the coherence-control cells; (v) restate the headline on absolute main effects with a pre-registered margin. None of these shrinks the study; together they turn a claim that a knowledgeable reviewer can falsify from the literature into one that has to be tested."""
